Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Gasoline Daily Market Analysis
China releases oil product new export quota
Indonesia seeks 90 RON gasoline via spot tender
The Asian gasoline complex was expected to soften on Oct. 30, amid the release of a fourth round of Chinese export quotas.
China has likely allocated 5.65 million mt of clean oil product export quotas in its fourth batch of the year, lifting the 2026 total by 1.9% year over year to 42.19 million mt, five sources with knowledge of the matter told Platts.
Market sources said quota availability was more than sufficient for the rest of the year, citing the 12.6% year-overyear export reductions in the first three quarters, lower refinery throughput expected in November and December, in addition to declining oil product inventories. Chinese refineries are required to prioritize domestic demand over export economics.
S&P Global Energy CERA analysts estimated that China would need to export at least 2 million barrels/day (around 7.72 million mt) of clean oil products over the final two months of the year to fully utilize the quotas. However, they added that oil product inventories were too low to support such export levels.
Brokers pegged the front-month FOB Singapore 92 RON gasoline swaps crack spread with Brent swaps in the $14.50$14.55/b range at 0300 GMT, down from the Platts assessment of $15.63/b at the Oct. 29 Asian close.
Broking sources also placed the physical FOB Singapore 92 RON gasoline crack against front-month ICE Brent crude oil futures in the $19.40-$19.45/b range at 0300 GMT Oct. 30, down from the Platts assessment of $20.77/b at the Oct. 29 Asian close, the data showed.
In tenders, Indonesia's Pertamina sought up to 600,000 barrels of 90 RON gasoline for loading and delivery over Nov. 16Dec. 4 via a tender that closed Oct. 29.

Singapore's gasoline exports fell 35.22% to 485,821 mt in the week ended Oct. 28, while gasoline imports more than doubled to 334,742 mt from 124,940 mt.
The decline in gasoline exports came despite higher outflows to Indonesia, the region's largest buyer of low-octane gasoline, which rose 17.43% week over week to 194,501 mt, the data showed.
Meanwhile, the surge in gasoline imports came as arrivals from India rose to 67,756 mt from none the previous week. Chinese gasoline inflows, however, edged down 0.53% week over week to 77,002 mt.
Net gasoline exports plunged 75.83% week over week to 151,079 mt in the week ended Oct. 28, the data showed.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Joshua Ong

Europe jet fuel/kerosene imports reach 1.5 million mt so far in October: CAS
Nigeria leads October supply at 329,000 mt Jet fuel, kerosene imports reach 1.5 million mt UK imports 499,000 mt jet fuel so far in October

Europe's jet fuel and kerosene imports have reached roughly 1.5 million metric tons during Oct. 1-27, compared with about 1.9 million mt imported during all of September, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data.
European jet fuel/kerosene imports in October so far include 329,000 mt from Nigeria, down from 342,000 mt in September; 214,000 mt from the US, down from 339,000 mt from last month; 195,000 mt from India, up from 68,000 mt in September; and 307,000 from South Korea, down from 571,000 in September.
The UK imported 499,000 mt of jet fuel during Oct. 1-27, compared with 692,000 mt in September, while Spain has taken in 304,000 mt in October so far, up from 181,000 mt seen last month, while France imported 255,000 mt, down from 321,000 mt in September.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the Jet CIF NWE Cargo price at $1,539.75/mt Oct. 29, down $53.50/mt day over day, while the Jet FOB FARAG barge price also decreased $53.50/ mt to $1,553.75/mt.

Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Jet Daily Market Analysis
Market structure flips to backwardation
Singapore's weekly jet fuel exports rise 78.27% to 89,633 mt
Asia-Pacific international air passenger traffic remains weak
The Asian jet fuel/kerosene market structure flipped back into backwardation Oct. 30 after shifting into contango for the first time in over a year, tracking strength in the crude complex amid heightened concerns over a potential escalation of the Middle East conflict following the US midterm elections.
Brokers pegged the front-month November-December FOB Singapore jet fuel/kerosene swap time spread -- an indicator of the near-term market outlook -- at plus 34 cents/b in midmorning Asian trading Oct. 30.
At the 0830 GMT Asian close Oct. 29, Platts assessed the spread at minus 17 cents/b, down from plus 86 cents/b the previous session.
The front-month market structure was last seen in contango June 2, 2025, and was last lower at minus 18 cents/b Oct. 3, 2024, according to Platts data.
The downswing came as China allocated a rare fourth tranche of clean oil product export quotas, likely consisting of

5.65 million metric tons, which would lift the 2026 total by 1.9% year over year to 42.19 million mt.
The release of additional export quotas will likely ease concerns about tightening Asian jet fuel/kerosene balances amid the upcoming peak winter season. Prior to the release of the fourth batch, market participants attributed uncertainty surrounding whether China's remaining quotas would suffice for the rest of the year as a source of bullishness for the complex.
Jet fuel/kerosene exports from China, including bonded supply and shipments to Hong Kong and Macau, typically reach 2 million mt or more per month, and remain vital in balancing the complex amid ongoing supply disruptions due to conflicts in the Middle East, sources said. This was evidenced by a rise in prices following the country's export suspensions in March and October.
In data news, Singapore's onshore commercial middle distillate stocks inched up 2.37% week over week to 8.5 million barrels over Oct. 22-28, data from Enterprise Singapore showed Oct. 29.
Jet fuel exports rose 78.27% week over week to 89,633 mt in the week to Oct. 28, as shipments to Australia, the top recipient, rose 34.29% to 33,394 mt.
The city-state exported 20,820 mt of jet fuel to the UK and 16,475 mt to the US.
The remaining outflows were absorbed by Mexico at 12,770 mt and the Netherlands at 6,170 mt.
Jet fuel imports fell by 13.95% week over week to 10,288 mt, with Japan contributing the bulk of the volumes at 10,283 mt.
Looking downstream, Asia-Pacific airlines' international passenger traffic inched up 0.7% year over year and fell 9.19% month over month to 30.4 million in September, the Association of Asia-Pacific Airlines said in a statement Oct. 29.
The marginal year-over-year increase came after five straight months of annual declines, AAPA said, coinciding with higher jet fuel prices driven by conflicts in the Middle East that continue to disrupt oil supplies.
"Performance remained uneven across markets, with capacity reductions contributing to lower passenger traffic on some routes, while longer-haul markets continued to grow, supported in part by passengers choosing routings that avoided Middle East hubs," the statement said.
According to aviation statistics company OAG, low-cost carriers in Asia have been more pressured to reduce capacities, particularly on short, price-sensitive routes within Southeast Asia and the wider Asia Pacific region.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Shu ling Lee

Diesel
TotalEnergies to cut French diesel pump prices following release of strategic stocks
France releases strategic diesel reserves
TotalEnergies cuts fuel costs at pumps
Heating oil demand rises amid cold weather
TotalEnergies announced Oct. 30 that it would be lowering the price of diesel at 705 of its Access pumping stations across France to 2.15/liter ($2.79/liter) starting Oct. 31. The network represents about 60% of TotalEnergies' fuel sales.
The retailer said it would also expand the discount to other stations in its Autoroute network from Nov. 2 as a result of

changing fundamentals in the global diesel market. TotalEnergies had previously said it would maintain a price cap
of 2.25 euros/liter ($2.52/liter) on diesel across its entire French network, which would remain unchanged.
The retailer also said that during France's strategic stock releases from Nov. 2 to Jan. 31, it would reduce costs for domestic heating gasoil by 200/cubic meter ($223.23/cubic meter) and by 20 euro cents/liter ($22.32/liter) on non-road diesel for use in agriculture, fishing and public works vehicles.
Platts assessed the 0.1% CIF Northwest European gasoil cargo flat price at $1,410/metric ton Oct. 29, and at a $26/mt discount to the front-month ICE LSGO futures contract.
In the 0.1% NWE gasoil market, market participants have recently said that demand is gradually picking up for the winter, as colder temperatures have come recently.
France remains one of the only Northwest European countries that still allows 1,000 ppm (0.1%) gasoil to be used as heating oil.
Europe's heating oil demand had been slow in the previous weeks heading into this winter, and buyers had been holding back on seasonal stock building as gasoil flat prices have roughly doubled from a year ago.
For comparison, the flat price of 0.1% CIF NWE gasoil cargoes has remained above $740.50/mt since Feb. 27 -- before the start of the US-Israel war with Iran -- and is also higher than the yearago level of $713.75/mt.
On the diesel side, Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the ULSD 10ppm CIF Northwest European diesel cargo flat price at $1,466/mt Oct. 29, and at a $30/mt premium to the frontmonth ICE LSGO futures contract.
-- David Neef, Patrick Mcallister

high logistical and storage costs, along with strong flat prices, push participants to limit buying volumes, according to distributors close to the matter.

ARA fuel oil stocks drop 18% week over week to 762,000 mt: Insights Global
VLSFO market tightens on stronger demand
HSFO resupply concerns continue amid high freight
Fuel oil stocks in the Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp refining hub fell 18.1% week over week to 762,000 mt in the seven days to Oct. 29, Insights Global data showed, with stocks drawing for a second consecutive week.
Fuel oil's share of overall oil product inventories in the ARA region dropped to 19%.
In the high sulfur fuel oil market, resupply concerns continued amid elevated dirty tanker freight rates.
Market participants expected high sulfur fuel oil premiums to edge higher as freight economics and refinery maintenance tightened prompt availability, even with transatlantic cargoes arriving.
The transatlantic arbitrage was open, one trader said, with "cargoes coming over from Canada, Mexico, Venezuela and the USGC."
A second Mediterranean-based trader agreed, noting that despite Western arrivals, "freight is still playing a role for sure" and that the market remained tight, "with maintenance at refineries across the basin and Latam barrels coming to the Med, which require blending."
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the front-month

HSFO crack spread at minus $10.335/b, down $1.653/b from minus $8.682/b the previous week.
In the Northwest European VLSFO market, lower stocks and higher local bunkering demand this week have contributed to some prompt tightness in the product, according to trader sources.
"Local demand is higher than usual," said a Northwest Europefocused trader, while a second ARA-focused source said that It looked "a bit tighter" in the VLSFO market.
In the Platts Market on Close assessment process, cash differentials for physical VLSFO rose this week into a low doubledigit range, highlighting this tightness.
A tighter VLSFO market has supported demand for blending 1% and mid-sulfur fuel oil products across the Northwest European market.
In the paper market, the front-month EU fuel oil 0.5%S marine fuel oil structure was assessed by Platts at a backwardation of $16/mt on Oct. 29, widening by $2.50/mt on the week. The 3.5%S fuel oil FOB Rotterdam barge front-month time spread equivalent was assessed at a backwardation of $20.75/mt on Oct. 29, narrowing by $3.50/mt.
In the retail bunker market, availability remained mixed, with market sources reporting a general tightness of HSFO on the cargo side, which could be reflected in bunker prices if it persists.
Demand remained weak, with very few spot inquiries due to high prices, traders said. Elevated prices remained supported by high freight, with traders not expecting much easing unless freight costs deflated.
-- Joseph Jaffe, Tommy Petrou, Iman Rezig, Nikos Batagiannis
Russia plans to extend diesel export ban until end of January: Novak
Some specific exemptions will be made
October diesel exports reach 510,000 mt
November targets 2.15 million mt shipments
Russia plans to extend the diesel export ban "with exceptions" until the end of January, Deputy Prime Minister Alexander Novak told Russian media Oct. 30.
The current ban applied to refiners expires Oct. 31. The ban on so-called non-producers, which include traders and small refineries that process less than 1 million metric tons/year, expires at the end of January 2027.
Earlier in October, Russia partially lifted the diesel export ban, after US President Donald Trump said that Russia had agreed to supply global markets amid concerns over supply shortages and high prices. The US Treasury Department's Office of Foreign Assets Control granted a sanctions waiver to Russia Oct. 9, allowing the sale, delivery, and import of Russian-origin diesel fuel, including imports into the US.
So far, under the sanctions waiver, Russia has shipped four cargoes of diesel, according to Novak. Novak has previously said that Russia could export 500,000 metric tons of diesel in October while fully meeting Russian domestic demand. Novak also said Oct. 30 that a resumption of Ukraine's attacks on Russian refineries "could lead to a reduction in supply volumes. The decrease is minor, but it is still less than what had been planned."

Russia was the second-largest global diesel exporter before the start of the country's fuel crisis this summer, triggered by Ukraine's attacks on refineries and other energy infrastructure.

European diesel/gasoil imports expected to reach 3.7 million mt in October
Imports to Europe set to reach 3.7 million mt in October
Around 606,000 mt of diesel/gasoil arrived on week
Europe's imports of diesel/gasoil are anticipated to reach 3.7 million metric tons in October, higher than the total of 3.4 million mt in September but lower than the total of 4.4 million mt

imported in October 2025, according to Kpler shipping data. Of this total, around 1.1 million mt is expected to come
from the US, lower than the 1.8 million mt that arrived from the US in September and the 1.4 million mt imported from the US in October 2025. All is expected to come from PADD 3 (US Gulf Coast), with none expected to come from PADD 1 (Atlantic Coast).
Meanwhile, 1.1 million diesel/gasoil mt is expected to come from India in October, far higher than the 573,000 mt in September, while 284,000 mt is expected to come from Saudi Arabia, down from 396,000 mt in September, according to Kpler.
Additionally, around 606,000 mt of diesel/gasoil was discharged in Europe in the week ended October 25, lower than the 1.2 million mt discharged the previous week, according to Kpler.
Meanwhile, Russian diesel and gasoil exports rose to their highest levels since June in the week ended Oct. 22, with Ukraine not targeting Russia's refineries since mid-October.
Most of the Russian diesel and gasoil -- about 224,000 mt -- was loaded in Primorsk in the Baltic Sea, up from 87,000 mt a week earlier. About 35,000 mt was loaded from Novorossiysk in the Black Sea, marking the first loadings from the port since the week starting Sept. 11. In addition, 67,000 mt was loaded in UstLuga in the Baltic Sea, for the first time since the week starting July 24.
Of these loadings, 75,000 mt of product was loaded for Turkey, up from 47,000 mt a week earlier, while 76,000 mt was loaded for the US and 35,000 mt was loaded for Egypt, up from no loading the previous week, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data. About 37,000 mt were destined for Benin in West Africa, with the remainder remaining unknown.
Turkey, Brazil, and Egypt were the main buyers of Russian diesel in recent years after the EU banned Russian fuel products in response to the country's full-scale invasion of Ukraine. Russia was the second-largest global diesel exporter before the start of the country's fuel crisis, triggered by Ukraine's attacks.
-- David Neef

Indian diesel/gasoil exports fall to 616,000 mt Indian jet fuel/kerosene rise to 86,000 mt

Over Oct. 1-27, Indian loadings of middle distillates stood at roughly 2.5 million mt, which is lower than the total of 3.1 million mt in September.
India's exports of diesel/gasoil fell to 616,000 mt, down from 626,000 mt the week prior, with the product heading to East Africa, Northwest Europe, the Mediterranean and East Coast South America.
The main recipients of Indian diesel/gasoil are expected to be the Netherlands, Spain and Tanzania, each taking around 100,000 mt, as well as Argentina taking 72,000 mt.
Overall, Indian diesel/gasoil loadings stood at roughly 2.2 million mt between Oct. 1-27, so far lower than the total volume of 2.6 million mt in September.

week ended Oct. 25, higher than the 40,000 mt in the previous week. Of the total, 65,000 mt was loaded for Spain and 21,000 mt for South Africa, according to CAS data.

Subsidy-driven FCA discounts mislead on Brazil diesel import economics
Government subsidy distorts diesel pricing
Russian supply tightens, narrows US gap
FCA discounts fail to reflect import costs
Brazil's diesel subsidy has complicated comparisons between prompt FCA prices and seaborne DAP cargo values, as government support is reflected differently in domestic spot prices and import-replacement economics.
"The basis is very difficult to identify in the FCA market now because of the subsidy," a market source said. Market estimates suggest around 2,400 reais/cubic meter is being reflected in FCA spot prices. "This is creating a very large discount to NYMEX contracts."
As of Oct. 30, the government had extended the diesel subsidy for another 30 days at 2,120 reais/cubic meter for domestic production and imports. Importers were eligible for an additional 1,400 reais/cubic meter benefit, due to expire Nov. 6 unless renewed. The government estimated the program's fiscal impact at about 10.5 billion reais.
Diesel spot prices fell Oct. 29 as offers began to reflect the renewed support, although trading remained thin. Platts assessed FCA Santos at 4,371.10 reais/cubic meter, equivalent to a 152.53 cents/gal discount to NYMEX December ULSD, while Paranagua was at 4,342.30 reais/cubic meter, or a 154.55 cents/ gal discount to NYMEX December ULSD.
Northern and Northeastern markets were firmer. Itaqui stood at 4,943 reais/cubic meter, reflecting a 112.46 cents/gal discount to NYMEX December ULSD, while Suape was at 4,957.40 reais/cubic meter, or a 111.45 cents/gal discount to NYMEX December ULSD.
Those FCA levels contrast with full-cargo DAP indications. For Itaqui, a US Gulf Coast cargo was indicated at a 1-3 cents/ gal discount to NYMEX December ULSD for Nov. 25-30 arrival. A shorter Nov. 10-15 window was indicated at a 10 cents/gal premium to NYMEX December ULSD, while another full cargo was heard at a 5 cents/gal premium to NYMEX December ULSD for Nov. 15-30 arrival. In South and Southeast Brazil, a Russian-origin MR cargo was indicated at a 10 cents/gal premium to NYMEX December ULSD for Nov. 15-25 arrival. In Paranagua, a 5,000-10,000 cubic-meter parcel

was offered at a 5 cents/gal premium to NYMEX December ULSD for Nov. 20-25 arrival.
"There is significant dispersion by origin, port and delivery window," the source said. "The subsidy complicates the FCA comparison, while the origin differential in the import market has become much smaller."
Russian availability still limited Brazilian ULSD replacement costs declined Oct. 29, while US-
origin and all-origin economics moved toward parity as tighter Russian availability eroded the discount previously associated with non-US supply.
For Nov. 13 arrival, the Platts physical curve placed USGC replacement costs at a 9.22 cents/gal premium to NYMEX December ULSD, compared with a 4.84 cents/gal premium to NYMEX December ULSD for all-origin supply, leaving a 4.38 cents/ gal gap.
The differential disappeared for Nov. 19 arrival, when both replacement costs reached a 3.66 cents/gal premium to NYMEX December ULSD, with the US becoming the most competitive origin.
Platts assessed the spread between all-origin and US-origin diesel at 1 cent/gal on Oct. 29, down from 11.25 cents/gal on Oct. 28. "The Russian discount has compressed because availability is tighter," the source said. "The US and Russian replacement curves are practically converging, with the US again becoming the more competitive origin."
Russian availability remained limited amid expectations that the country could extend diesel export restrictions through yearend, although individual companies could continue exporting under quotas to meet existing obligations.
The market therefore faces two simultaneous dynamics: government support is contributing to deep FCA discounts to NYMEX December ULSD, while the narrowing Russian-origin discount is bringing US-origin and all-origin DAP replacement costs closer together.
FCA prices represent nationalized product available for prompt withdrawal, while DAP indications reflect future cargoes priced according to origin, destination, volume and arrival window. The subsidy adds another variable because its effect is reflected

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

27

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 30, 2026

differently across the domestic and import markets. The FCA discount to NYMEX December ULSD therefore should
not be interpreted as a directly executable DAP cargo basis. Physical DAP indications provide a clearer signal of the marginal cost of replacing imported diesel supply.
-- Renan Araujo, Kauanna Navarro
Platts Asia and Middle Eastern Gasoil Daily Market Analysis
Indonesia's Pertamina seeks gasoil for Nov delivery
Singapore's gasoil exports at near five-month high
Sentiment in the Asian ultra-low sulfur gasoil market was weighed down Oct. 30 by a longer supply outlook from China and South Korea.
Brokers pegged the front-month November-December Singapore gasoil swaps time spread at $1.83/barrel in midafternoon Asian trading Oct. 30, compared with $1.32/b at the 0830 GMT Asian close Oct. 29.
In the physical segment, the benchmark Platts-assessed cash differential for FOB Singapore gasoil with a maximum sulfur content of 10 parts per million plunged 53 cents/b day over day to 63 cents/b at the 0830 GMT Asian close Oct. 29.
The South Korean government said it will permit additional diesel exports from November, provided that refiners fulfill domestic supply obligations and maintain adequate inventories, according to an Oct. 29 statement from the Ministry of Trade, Industry and Resources.
The export cap had been widened to 110% of the corresponding month a year earlier, from the existing ceiling of 100%, according to the trade ministry.
China had released a fourth allocation of clean oil product export quotas estimated at 5.6 million mt, lifting the 2026 total by 1.6% year over year to 42.06 million metric tons.
"More export availability from South Korea, in tandem with the Chinese new export quotas, would pressure the regional market," an Asia-based middle distillates trader said.
In tenders, Indonesia's Pertamina Patra Niaga sought up to 3 million barrels of 2,500 ppm sulfur gasoil for delivery over November to Makassar, Tanjung Uban, Tuban, Baubau, IBT Pulau Laut, Wayame, Medan, Teluk Kabung, Tanjung Gerem and Semarang. The tender closes on Oct. 30, with same-day validity.
Japan's Cosmo Oil had offered 300,000 barrels of 10 ppm sulfur gasoil loading over Nov. 26-30 from Sakai via a tender that closed on Oct. 29.
Singapore's onshore commercial middle distillate stocks inched up 2.37% week over week to 8.5 million barrels over Oct. 22-28, data from Enterprise Singapore showed Oct. 29.
The city-state remained a net exporter of gasoil in the week ended Oct. 28, as outflows surged 44.20% to a nearly five-month high of 529,282 mt. Exports were last higher over June 11-17, at 561,453 mt, historical data showed.
Outflows to Australia and Malaysia, the two largest

destinations, jumped to 117,830 mt and 105,864 mt, respectively, compared with 78,492 mt and 37,268 mt a week ago.
Notably, Singapore shipped 79,615 mt of gasoil to the Netherlands, despite expensive clean tanker freight rates, according to Asia-based market participants.
In contrast, imports plunged 44.20% on the week to 84,985 mt over Oct. 22-28, Enterprise Singapore data showed.
Inflows from China nearly halved to 41,310 mt, compared with 82,035 mt the previous week.
Singapore also received 43,564 mt from Thailand, and a mere 108 mt from South Korea, the data showed.
-- Mei huey Ng

Ukraine resumes drone strikes on Russian refineries
Russia extends diesel export ban to January
The European diesel complex strengthened Oct. 30, following the resumption of Ukrainian drone strikes on Russian energy infrastructure.
Platts assessed the underlying front-month ICE low sulfur gasoil futures contract up $43/metric ton to $1,479/mt on Oct. 30, which drove up the physical complex.
The price comes amid resumed hostilities between Russia and Ukraine.
The Rosneft-owned Novokuybishev refinery in Russia's Samara Oblast came under Ukrainian attack on Oct. 30, according to reports by Russian media outlet Astra.
Astra cited photos sent by readers and noted that its OSINT analyst determined that a fire had broken out at the 164,000 barrels/day refinery. The last time the Novokuybishev refinery came under attack was Aug. 22.
Ukrainian drone producer Fire Point said in a Telegram post that the refinery was attacked using its drones but did not clarify the source of the information about the strikes.
Russia's Deputy Prime Minister Alexander Novak confirmed to Vesti news Oct. 30 that attacks on Russian refineries had been renewed, with three refineries targeted overnight.
"There was a pause for a while and today attacks on the refineries were renewed," he said in comments broadcast by Vesti.
There were no confirmed reports of Ukraine attacking Russian refineries from Oct. 16 to Oct. 29, marking the longest strike-free period for Russia's refining system in more than seven months.
The pause in strikes on refineries followed major pressure from the US administration on Ukraine's President Volodymyr Zelenskyy. US President Donald Trump has repeatedly blamed Ukraine for keeping diesel prices high.

Platts Asia and Middle East Fuel Oil Daily Market Analysis
LSFO crack against Brent highest in over four years
Benchmark 380 CST HSFO cash premium inches up
Singapore fuel oil stocks hit two-week low as exports rise
The Asian low sulfur fuel oil market structure was seen firmer Oct. 30, after the refining margins for the marine fuel surged to its strongest level in more than four years amid tight supplies.
The Singapore marine fuel 0.5%S November-December swaps time spread was pegged at $42.25/metric ton in midafternoon Asian trading Oct. 30, compared with the Platts assessment of the spread at $41.25/mt at the Asian close Oct. 29.
The Singapore front-month crack spread for the 0.5%S marine fuel grade against ICE Brent futures was assessed 87 cents/ barrel higher day over day at $29.80/b on Oct. 29, climbing to its highest since July 5, 2022, when it was assessed at $30.82/b, Platts data showed. The front-month refining margin for November was pegged further up at $30.70/b in midafternoon Asian trading Oct. 30.
Limited arbitrage arrivals of blending components are expected to keep the regional market tight in the near term, according to trade sources.
Despite a record-high East-West spread, steep freight rates were dampening arbitrage opportunities, limiting supplies, trade sources said. Platts assessed the spread between Singapore marine fuel 0.5%S cargo and FOB Rotterdam 0.5%S barge assessments, or the East-West spread, at $192.50/mt on Oct. 29, its widest on record, according to Platts data dating back to April 2022.
Platts assessed the Singapore marine fuel 0.5%S cargo's

differential over the Mean of Platts Singapore marine fuel 0.5%S assessments at a premium of $31.16/mt at the Asian close Oct. 29, slipping from $31.42/mt in the preceding session, weighed down by a competitive offer from BP during the Platts Market on Close assessment process.
"If you are an ex-wharf supplier, then I think there are not many changes in the blending margins. But, if you are selling cargo, maybe your margins will be depressed because the cargo premium is very low as one needs to compensate for the high freight and high cross-harbor charges," said one Singaporebased trader.
Meanwhile, the Singapore 380 CST HSFO NovemberDecember swaps time spread was pegged at $61.25/mt in midafternoon Asian trading Oct. 30, against a backwardation of $58.25/mt at the Asian close Oct. 29.
Platts assessed the Singapore 380 CST HSFO cargo cash differential over the MOPS 380 CST HSFO assessment at $48.97/ mt at the Asian close Oct. 29, up 7 cents/mt from Oct. 28. The benchmark HSFO cash premium has gained 0.7% so far in the week that started Oct. 26, Platts data showed.
Singapore's commercial stockpiles of heavy distillates dipped 0.9% week over week to a two-week low of 18.68 million barrels in the week ended Oct. 28, according to Enterprise Singapore data released late Oct. 29, partly due to a rise in exports from the Southeast Asian hub.
The city-state's fuel oil exports increased 5% from the preceding week to 462,542 mt in the week ended Oct. 28, as the city-state exported 23,511 mt of fuel oil to Bangladesh after a two-week hiatus, and about 71,523 mt to South Korea in the latest week, compared with none the week earlier, the data showed.
Singapore also imported 99,989 mt of fuel oil from Russia in the week to Oct. 28, for the first time in 17 weeks, but there were also no arrivals from Europe in the latest week, the data showed.

Asia naphtha prices surge to six-month high on crude spike, tight supply
Naphtha hits $1,082.25/mt on higher crude prices
West of Suez November flow down 29% on month
CFR Japan cash differential at hghest in 2 months
Asian naphtha prices reached a six-month high Oct. 30, tracking a spike in crude prices as renewed Middle East tensions heightened concerns over constrained regional supply.
The benchmark physical C+F Japan naphtha price was assessed at $1,082.25/metric ton at the Oct. 30 Asian close, up $33/mt day over day, reaching its highest level since April 30, when it was assessed at $1,120.75/mt.
The rise was largely driven by stronger crude prices. Crude prices spike following Yemen-based Houthi militants claimed a series of fresh missile strikes on Saudi Arabian energy facilities, including Abqaiq and two refineries, group spokesperson Ameen Hayyan said on X late Oct. 29.
The Houthis claimed strikes on the 400,000 barrels/day Rabigh refinery and the 550,000 b/d Ras Tanura refinery, as well as Aramco facilities in Abqaiq, without specifying what was targeted.
Market participants were also monitoring the risk of a further escalation in Middle East tensions following the US midterm elections.
"Market looks a bit short for Nov and Dec," a trader said, as

persistently high freight rates continued to constrain arbitrage flows from the West of Suez into Asia.
According to S&P Global Commodities at Sea data, West of Suez arbitrage arrivals into the Far East were estimated at around 1.363 million mt for November, down from 1.909 million mt in October.
Meanwhile, heightened Middle East tensions raised concerns over potential disruptions to tanker traffic through the Strait of Hormuz, which could further curb Middle Eastern naphtha flows to Asia.
Cash differentials have also strengthened alongside with tighter supply. Most recently, Crasus Chemical bought 25,000 mt of open-specification naphtha for first-half December delivery at a premium ranging from the high $50s/mt to around $60/mt to Mean of Platts Japan naphtha assessments, CFR, with pricing over the 30 days prior to delivery. The tender closed Oct. 29.
Platts assessed the CFR Japan cash differential against the benchmark Mean of Platts Japan naphtha was assessed at $57/mt at the Oct. 30 Asian close, a two-month high. It was last assessed higher at $65/mt on Aug. 4.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Zoey Ng

Platts European Naphtha Daily Market Analysis
Crack strengthens
ARA stocks plunge 25.62% in one week
East-West spread widens $8/mt to $82/mt
The European naphtha market crack strengthened Oct. 30 amid a spiking front-month crack spread and stronger East-West spreads.
The naphtha crack strengthened on propane-linked paper flows, as buying in the propane-naphtha spread prompted followon naphtha buying, with traders legging out of positions.
"[The crack increase] was on the back of strong propane buying today," a naphtha paper market player said. "Real guys [were] selling the propane-naphtha [spread] in highs, shavers [were] buying it, then buying the naphtha after to leg out of it."
Steam cracker demand for naphtha has strengthened as constrained propane availability pushes some flexible feedstock

demand back toward naphtha, helping insulate the market from weakness elsewhere in the refined products complex.
On the physical side, European naphtha fundamentals remained supported by tight prompt availability and strong petrochemical buying, with healthy eastbound arbitrage adding further pressure on regional supply.
Naphtha Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp stocks declined sharply by 25.62% week over week to 270,000 metric tons. The draw extended the year-over-year deficit, with inventories now 43.04% below the 474,000 mt recorded in late October 2025.
European gasoline-naphtha spreads have continued to fall sharply as weakening gasoline fundamentals and tighter naphtha availability eroded blending economics, with forward structures signaling continued pressure into early 2027.
Gasoline market participants have pointed to healthy refinery runs and ample availability of finished-grade gasoline, while immediate export opportunities have remained limited. One market source said cargoes remained available with no real scarcity of gasoline, despite the recent weakness in cracks and market structure.
Meanwhile, softer gasoline blending demand has freed up some barrels for the chemical sector, but robust cracker buying continues to underpin prompt naphtha values.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, assessed the physical naphtha CIF NWE cargo price at $1,005/metric ton, down $34.75/ mt day over day.
Platts assessed the front-month CIF NWE naphtha swap up $38/mt at $982.50/mt, while the crack was up 95 cents/barrel at minus $2.95/b.
Platts assessed the November/December time spread at $46/mt, up $3.50/mt day over day, while the December/January spread was up $3/mt at $30/mt.
The front-month East-West spread -- the spread between the CFR Japan naphtha cargo swap and the CIF NWE equivalent -- was at $82/mt, up $8/mt day over day.
-- Barbara Fernandez-pita

US leads European LPG imports as Belgium tops destinations
Belgium receives 259,700 mt, leads imports
Propane comprises 70% of total volumes
Algeria supplies 307,300 mt, ranks second
The US dominated LPG supply into the European and Mediterranean destinations in October, with Belgium emerging

as the largest receiving market, according to S&P Global Commodities at Sea, or CAS.
Approximately 1.6 million metric tons of LPG were recorded across 119 international shipments discharging during the month to date.
Propane accounted for 70% of total volumes, while the distribution of arrivals highlighted the importance of both Northwest European import hubs and Mediterranean destinations.
The US ranked as the largest export origin, supplying 765,100 mt, more than double the volume from second-ranked Algeria at 307,300 mt.
Norway followed with 221,500 mt. Together, the three leading origins supplied approximately 1.29 million mt, underlining the concentration of deliveries among a relatively small group of producers.
The prominence of US-origin cargoes illustrated the importance of transatlantic supply to the regional balance. Algeria and Norway provided substantial complementary volumes, highlighting the contribution of North African and North Sea supply alongside longer-haul imports.
Belgium led the receiving countries with 259,700 mt, followed by the Netherlands at 239,500 mt and Turkey at 232,500 mt. France ranked fourth with 204,500 mt, ahead of Italy at 164,900 mt. Combined arrivals into the five largest destinations reached approximately 1.1 million mt. Belgium and the Netherlands together received 499,200 mt.
Substantial arrivals into Turkey, France and Italy also highlighted the Mediterranean's importance within the selected trade flows.
By commodity grade, CAS recorded approximately 1.1 million mt of C3 propane and 462,500 mt of C4 butane. Propane represented roughly seven-tenths of the combined grade volumes shown.
-- Baran Serdaroglu

Platts European Propane Daily Commentary
US propane cargoes remain closely held
CIF large cargo prices jump $40.75/mt
November buying appetite remains weak
Northwest European propane availability remained constrained on Oct. 30, with limited regional supply and closely held US volumes keeping attention on replacement cargoes ahead of winter.
"Everyone is keeping close their US supplies," a market participant said, highlighting the difficulty of securing additional spot volumes despite Europe's dependence on transatlantic imports.
Recent US arrivals had offered some relief, although access to further cargoes remained important to the regional balance.
Competition for export availability limited the scope for buyers to assume that additional requirements could be covered readily.
"Crazy tight in [Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp]," the participant said.
However, despite tight market conditions, some market participants indicated weak November buying appetite.
Recent offers for first-half December delivery, below equivalent November levels, suggested that "nobody wants to pay up for November tonnes," a trader said.
Elevated natural gas costs continued to support propane consumption as a fuel, while the start of seasonal heating requirements added to demand.
Petrochemical buying remained sensitive to propane's competitiveness against naphtha.
Inland logistics also remained a constraint. Although Rhine conditions had improved, restricted barge loads maintained reliance on truck and rail deliveries, limiting the pace at which available coastal supply could reach inland consumers.
One bid was observed in the CIF propane cargo market. BASF offered on a TOT23 cargo, CIF Antwerp, for H1 December delivery, at a 100% CIF Amsterdam-Rotterdam-Antwerp large December swap plus $27/mt. It was not deemed competitive, and therefore, the assessment was based on trader sentiment.
Platts assessed the CIF large cargo market in outright terms at $908.25/metric ton, up $40.75/mt day over day, at a $23.25/mt premium over the propane CIF November swap, down $4.25/mt from premium levels.
In the propane coaster market, no bids, offers or trades were heard in the MOC. In the absence of competitive indications, the market was assessed on trader sentiment.
Platts assessed the propane coaster market at $968/mt, up $40/mt day over day, at a $59.75/mt premium to large cargoes.
In the propane barge market, no bids, offers, or trades were heard in the MOC. In the absence of competitive indications, the market was assessed on trader sentiment.

Platts European Butane Daily Commentary
Butane supply tightens in Northwest Europe
Prices surge up to $46/mt amid shortages
Winter gasoline blending drives demand higher
Northwest European butane remained supported by restricted merchant availability and seasonal gasoline blending requirements, with refinery retention limiting volumes reaching the spot market on Oct. 30.
A market participant called the Amsterdam-RotterdamAntwerp hub "crazy tight," so the Mediterranean "can only be worse."
The transition to winter-grade gasoline supported butane consumption within refinery blending systems.
Greater internal use reduced the surplus available to external buyers, leaving merchant blenders competing for a limited pool of spot supply.
Butane's strength also restricted its attractiveness to petrochemical consumers. Buying interest from that sector remained sensitive to alternative feedstock economics, with seasonal blending requirements providing the stronger source of support.
Replacement supply remained important, although limited regional availability and firm US-origin economics complicated procurement. Buyers consequently remained focused on the timing and availability of suitable parcels.
Inland transport constraints added to distribution challenges. Restricted barge loading maintained demand for alternative delivery routes, while improving water levels had yet to translate into a full recovery in carrying capacity.
In the butane CIF NWE large cargo market, no bids, offers, or trades were heard in the Platts Market on Close assessment process. In the absence of competitive indications, the market was assessed on trader sentiment.
Platts assessed the CIF large cargo market in outright terms at $1,125.50/metric ton, up $38.75/mt day over day and stable as a percentage to Platts CIF NWE naphtha at 112%.
In the absence of indications in the MOC, the butane coaster market was assessed on trader sentiment.
Platts assessed the FOB Seagoing butane coaster market at $1,327/mt, up $46/mt day over day and stable as a percentage to Platts CIF NWE naphtha at 132%.
Platts assessed the CIF seagoing market at $1,186/mt, up $41/ mt day over day and stable as a percentage to Platts CIF NWE naphtha at 118%.
This put the FOB coasters at a $141/mt premium over the CIF coaster market.
Platts is part of S&P Global Energy. -- Baran Serdaroglu

Platts Western Mediterranean LPG Daily Commentary
West Med LPG supply tightens on demand
Butane prices jump $55/mt to $1,307/mt
November imports may ease December costs
West Mediterranean LPG availability remained tight on Oct. 30, with participants highlighting procurement challenges across both propane and butane as attention turned to November requirements.
"The market is very tight in propane as well," a market participant said, indicating that supply concerns extended beyond the butane market.
The participant described current spot and term prices as "crazy high," underlining concerns over replacement costs for buyers exposed to Mediterranean supply. Elevated purchasing costs added to the difficulty of securing competitively priced volumes in an already constrained market.
Butane availability remained supported by seasonal gasoline blending demand, with refinery retention reducing the volumes available to merchant buyers. Limited alternative supply left consumers dependent on the timing of fresh offers and cargo arrivals.
Tightness in Northwest Europe also limited the potential for that region to provide relief. Competition for available molecules consequently remained an important consideration for Mediterranean procurement.
Additional imports arriving by the end of November could, however, provide scope for a correction in December, a trader added.
A recovery in availability could ease prompt pressure and temper market strength, although the timing and volume of arrivals remained critical to any sustained easing.
Petrochemical demand remained sensitive to feedstock economics, while fuel and blending requirements continued to underpin buying interest ahead of winter.
Platts assessed the West Mediterranean FOB butane market at $1,307/metric ton, up $55/mt day over day and up 1 percentage point versus Platts CIF NWE naphtha at 130%, or at a $20/mt discount to FOB Northwest Europe coasters.

Tankers
Hormuz traffic ticks up, but ship attacks continue
26 ships transit Hormuz on Oct. 29
Five crude tankers transit strait
Iran's Guards report tanker incidents
Traffic through the Strait of Hormuz totaled 26 ships on Oct. 29, including five crude tankers moving into the waterway, according to the latest report from S&P Global Commodities At Sea. The count ticked up from 18 transits the day before.
Ship attacks have picked up over the past few weeks in the contested waterway, and four incidents were reported by Iranian media Oct. 29 and one incident on Oct. 30.
Two oil tankers sailing with their tracking systems switched off were involved in accidents on the western and eastern sides of the Strait of Hormuz while attempting to transit via what Iran deems an unauthorized route, Iran's Revolutionary Guards navy said in an Oct. 30 statement carried by state news agency IRNA.
The vessels entered an unsafe area after ignoring repeated radio warnings and subsequently stopped sailing, the statement said.
Iran also said Oct. 30 that an oil tanker hit a mine in the strait, semi-official Fars news reported. Oman's Royal Navy said it evacuated 25 crew members from a Panama-flag vessel after a fire broke out on board the ship that was 106 nautical miles offshore its northern Musandam province that juts into the Strait of Hormuz.
UK Maritime Trade Operations said Oct. 29 that a tanker transiting inbound into the strait just off the coast of Khasab, Oman, was on fire after a small boat approached the tanker and attached two explosive devices to its hull. The fire was extinguished. Around the tip of the peninsula, UKMTO reported another vessel, a dhow, on fire the same day.
"IRGC attacks, attempted attacks, and/or harassment activity persisted, including UAV overflight, targeted surveillance of merchant shipping, and occasional VHF hailing," the latest Joint Maritime Information Center advisory said.
Iranian-linked and sanctioned vessels continued to move through the Strait of Hormuz Oct. 29, with CAS reporting 10 such ships made the journey that day.
Just beyond the Strait of Hormuz, activity across the US naval blockade line eased to 55 transits on Oct. 29, comprising 28 inbound and 27 outbound movements, the report said. Two Iranian-linked vessels crossed the US blockade line moving toward the strait.
Across the Arabian Peninsula at the other maritime chokepoint, the Bab al-Mandab Strait, 31 vessels moved through the waterway, including nine tankers.
-- Lauren Holtmeier, Aresu Eqbali

LR1 rates hold amid availability gap
Gulf MR freight edges up on tight supply
The East of Suez LR tanker cargo inquiries in the Gulf of Oman remained muted Oct. 30, with ATS' loading Nov. 8 on the STS Sohar-Japan awaiting coverage as the market looked for a fresh fixture to test prevailing levels.
Two LR1s were heard released by a Middle Eastern supplier for end-October loading on the STS Oman-Japan route. Charterers were heard seeking lower LR1 rates, while owners continued to hold around the key psychological level of w500, according to market sources.
Differences between owners and charterers persisted amid uncertainty surrounding shuttle tanker availability.
"There has been some difference in expectations between owners and charterers because of uncertainty around shuttle tanker availability. Charterers are looking for ships on a prompt basis, which is encouraging owners to seek a premium," an LR broker said.
For LR2s, some backhaul activity was heard emerging from North Asia, with several LR2s heard placed on subjects for cargoes loading in the region.
China has likely allocated 5.65 million metric tons of clean oil product export quotas in its fourth batch of the year, lifting the 2026 total by 1.9% year over year to 42.19 million mt, according to five sources with knowledge of the matter.
Market sources said the additional export quotas could provide some support for backhaul demand.
Medium Range, or MRs, saw diverging trends across key regions on Oct. 30. Several brokers said the relaxation of South Korea's export restrictions and the boost in Chinese export quota should support vessel demand in North Asia in the near term.
In fixtures, the Oriental Greenstone was placed on subjects at w650 by Ampol for the Nov. 9 loading on the South KoreaAustralia route, sources said.
Southeast Asia MRs were assessed lower, amid easing supply in the region and limited outstanding cargoes, market participants said. The Unique Harmony was placed on subjects at w530 by BP for the Nov. 13 loading on the SingaporeAustralia route, sources said. Several brokers noted that upcoming transactions on the same route are likely to see lower rates.
Gulf MRs edged higher. With supply continuing to tighten and at least four outstanding cargoes reported, there is potential for freight to increase, several brokers said. The Torm Supreme was placed on subjects at w570 by Reliance for the Nov. 11 loading on the Sikka-East Africa route, sources said.

Private fixing and Brazil demand lift VLCCs

WAF Suezmax freight drops

Platts Americas Clean Tanker Daily Commentary
MR tanker rates drop on low activity
Some expect rates to drop further next week
Americas Medium Range clean tanker rates declined further Oct. 30 amid continued low market activity, which allowed tonnage availability on the US Gulf Coast to increase.
There were five tankers available for prompt loading in the region, with an additional 20 units, on average, in the standard three- to 10-day loading window, according to shipbroker position lists.
Some market sources expected the bearish trend to continue during the first days of the following week before rates could begin to increase again, as tanker availability was not considered particularly ample when taking expected public and private cargoes into account.
"It doesn't seem like very many people are ballasting in yet," a shipbroker said. "I think the list is pretty balanced. I wouldn't be surprised if a few guys start the week out at slightly lower numbers and then it firms right back up."
The declines seen during the day followed a recent fixture done Oct. 29, in which SOL placed the Torm Birgitte on subjects

for a Nov. 3-5 loading USGC-Caribbean run at $1.65 million. This represented a $150,000 decline for the short-haul routes,
bringing the USGC-Caribbean route to $1.65 million and the USGC-East Coast Mexico voyage to $1.35 million.
The decline on the short-haul routes also led to expectations for lower levels on the longer routes. Active market indications for the transatlantic runs continued to fall below the last-done w500 mark, ranging between w450 and w500, with the bulk heard below the w475 level.
Platts assessed rates for the benchmark 38,000 mt USGC-UK Continent run at w470, down w25 day over day, narrowing the rate spread with the USGC-Brazil run to w75, with rates for the latter assessed at w545.
The lump sum long-haul routes ended the week without any freight tests reported, prompting market calls for the benchmark USGC-Chile run to remain within a wide range of $4.5 million to $4.8 million. The bulk of indications moved lower in line with the overall bearish sentiment seen during the final days of the week.
Platts assessed freight for the USGC-Chile route at $4.7 million, down $50,000 day over day.

BRAZIL DATA: September refinery output lower as harvests end
Harvest seasons peak in September
Agriculture demand trends lower
Gasoline mixed, jet fuel declines
Brazil's oil refineries produced fewer barrels of diesel, gasoline and other refined products in September as the country's massive oil seed and sugarcane harvest seasons started to fade, reducing domestic demand from the agriculture sector, according to the latest production report from the National Petroleum Agency.
Brazil's refineries produced 66.075 million barrels of diesel, gasoline and other refined products in September, down 0.8% from 66.611 million barrels in September 2025, the agency (ANP) said Oct. 29. September's refinery output also sank 2.5% from 67.744 million barrels in August.
Refinery output trended lower in September as the peak phase of the oil seed and sugarcane harvest seasons completed, the ANP report showed. Reduced demand from the agriculture sector undermined the need for barrels of diesel to power trucks and tractors. The harvest season typically starts in March-April each year, depending on weather conditions. Peak gathering takes place in September, with the harvest winding down in November before a fallow period in December-February each cycle.
Refined product output also has been supported by ongoing government price subsidies and tax breaks, which were aimed at offsetting the impact of volatile international oil and refined product prices on Latin America's largest economy. The subsidies were recently extended, but tax breaks will likely end in 2027.
Brazil produced 26.954 million barrels of diesel in September, up 0.6% from 26.78 million barrels in September 2025, the ANP said. Diesel production also was up 0.8% from 26.743 million barrels in August.
Diesel is Brazil's top refined product in terms of consumption, according to the ANP.
Gasoline output was mixed, according to the ANP. Gasoline is Brazil's No. 2 refined product in terms of consumption.
Refineries produced 15.036 million barrels of gasoline in September, down 2.5% from 15.418 million barrels in September 2025, the ANP said. September's gasoline production, however, advanced 1.6% from 14.796 million barrels in August.
Jet fuel production, meanwhile, was broadly lower despite a holiday-related increase in air travel demand, the ANP report showed. Brazil's refineries produced 2.844 million barrels of jet fuel, down 2.9% from 2.928 million barrels in September 2025. September's jet fuel output also fell 10.9% from 3.192 million barrels in August.

Repar shutdown in October Brazil's refinery output will be undermined in October after
Petrobras started a weekslong maintenance shutdown at the Repar refinery in Parana state on Oct. 5, according to the company. The 207,000 b/d will be idled as part of a $150 million project to work on the unit's Fluid Catalytic Cracker, or FCC. The refinery's diesel and gasoline hydrotreatment units will also undergo maintenance.
More than 1,000 pieces of equipment will be subject to inspection, maintenance and interventions, according to Petrobras. The work is expected to be completed in the second half of November.
The shutdown is part of Petrobras' wider expansion efforts that aim to expand overall refinery output to meet about 85% of Brazil's diesel consumption by 2030, according to the company. Under Petrobras' $109 billion strategic investment plan for 20262030, the company earmarked $21 billion for investments in its refineries to raise processing capacity by about 320,000 b/d. The investment outlay included $2.4 billion for maintenance projects.
Petrobras will likely further increase refinery investments as part of its 2027-2031 strategic plan, with Petrobras CEO Magda Chambriard saying Sept. 25 that the company will work to meet 100% of Brazil's diesel needs by 2031 in order to improve the country's energy security and tamp down inflation. Diesel is Brazil's top refined product in terms of consumption, with Latin America's largest economy typically importing about 25% of domestic demand each year.
-- Jeff Fick
BRAZIL DATA: September refined product sales diverge as harvest seasons close
Peak harvest in September
Extended diesel subsidies
Hydrous ethanol sales rise
Sales of diesel, gasoline, and other refined products were mixed in September as the peak of the country's oilseed and sugarcane harvest seasons wound down, reducing demand from the agriculture sector, according to the latest monthly sales report from the National Petroleum Agency, or ANP.
Fuel distributors sold 87.252 million barrels of refined products in September, up 0.4% from 86.907 million barrels in September 2025, the ANP said Oct. 29. September's refined production sales, however, fell 1.3% from 88.369 million barrels in August.
Refined product sales started to see reduced support from the agriculture industry, with the oilseed and sugarcane harvest seasons typically peaking in September. The agriculture sector is a key pillar of demand, making diesel Brazil's top refined product in terms of consumption, according to the ANP.
State-led oil company Petrobras, which is the country's top refiner, produced record diesel volumes of 769,000 b/d in the third quarter to meet demand, the company said Oct. 28. Refinery

Brazil sold 12.737 million barrels of hydrous ethanol in September, up 15.6% from 11.022 million barrels in September 2025, the ANP said. September's sales, however, were down 1.5% from 12.929 million barrels in August.

India's Reliance's Q2 throughput increases 13.8% in Q2 FY2027
Refined product Q2 sales up 16% over Q1
Product Q2 sales rise 16% quarter over quarter
Feedstock prices drag gains in product prices
India's Reliance Industries Ltd.'s total throughput at its Jamnagar integrated refinery petrochemical complex rose 13.8% quarter over quarter to 20.6 million mt in the July-September period, or the second quarter of its fiscal year 2027, the company said Oct. 30, reflecting operational resilience against the backdrop of geopolitical tensions.
Reliance's Q2 throughput was 1% down from a year ago, the company said.
"Our O2C (Oil to Chemicals) business delivered a particularly strong performance this quarter, led by tight fuel markets, improved downstream petrochemical deltas and firm domestic demand," said Mukesh D. Ambani, chairman and managing director, Reliance Industries Limited, said in an earnings release.
The ability to optimize feedstock sourcing and product

placement along with operations across the value chain enabled Reliance to improve margin capture, Ambani said.
"This performance underscores the strength and resilience of our O2C business," Ambani said in a statement.
Reliance sold 18.1 million mt of refined oil products in Q2, up 16% over the April-June period or Q1, it said.
The second quarter sales of refined products were the same as recorded in the first quarter, the company said.
Reliance operates the world's largest refinery complex at Jamnagar in Gujarat, on the west coast of India, according to the company's website.
The company's domestic-focused refinery has a capacity of 33 million mt/year (660,000 b/d) while the export-oriented unit has a capacity of 35.2 million mt/year (704,000 b/d), the company said.
Reliance's Jio-bp retail network rose to 2,304 outlets in Q2 compared with 2,057 a year ago, it said.
In Q2, global oil demand fell by 3.4 million b/d year on year to 102.5 million b/d, the company said, and added diesel demand fell by 0.35 million b/d, gasoline demand fell by 0.3 million b/d and jet fuel demand fell by 0.13 million b/d.
Global refinery crude throughput was lower by 3.5 million b/d on the year at 80.9 million b/d in 2Q FY26, they said, reflecting the impact of attacks on refining infrastructure in the Middle East and Russia.
Gas output In Q2, production from the Krishna-Godavari basin, popularly
known as the KG-D6 block, was 57.8 Bcf (Reliance's share) compared to 63.5 Bcf a year ago, Reliance said, reflecting the impact of natural decline in production from the oil and gas block on the east coast.
In Q2, the average production from the KG-D6 block stood at 24 million cubic meters/day of gas and 15,903 b/d of oil/ condensate, it said.
Reliance realized an average gas price for the KG-D6 block of $8.88/MMBtu in Q2 compared with $9.97/MMBtu a year ago, it said.
Petrochemicals In Q2, domestic polymer demand declined by 3.5% year over
year with limited product availability due to supply disruptions and elevated product prices, Reliance said.
Downstream demand was also impacted by seasonal monsoon weakness and need-based buying in the agriculture, furniture and household sectors, it said.
Domestic polyester demand declined by 6.1% in 2Q FY27, reflecting global economic uncertainties, price volatility and cautious buying, it said.
PET demand growth was healthy at 5.3%, supported by beverage-sector demand with a weaker monsoon, it added.
In Q2, polyester chain margins recovered sharply, reaching a 3-year high level, led by paraxylene and purified terephthalic acid deltas, Reliance said.
Reliance said downstream chemical deltas witnessed

Ukraine resumes attacks on Russian refineries: reports
Fire at 164,000 b/d Novokuybishev plant: Astra
US pressure halted strikes for two weeks
Sanctions waiver permits Russian diesel sales
The Rosneft-owned Novokuybishev refinery in Russia's Samara Oblast came under Ukrainian attack on Oct. 30, according to reports by Russian media outlet Astra.
Astra cited photos sent by readers and noted that its OSINT analyst determined that a fire had broken out at the 164,000 barrels/day refinery. The last time the Novokuybishev refinery came under attack was on Aug. 22.
Ukrainian drone producer Fire Point said in a Telegram post that the refinery was attacked using its drones but did not clarify the source of the information about the strikes.
The administration of the Samara Oblast confirmed there was a drone attack on the region in a post on Russian social media platform Max, but did not comment on the refinery strike specifically.
The office of the Ukrainian president was not immediately available for comment. Rosneft did not reply to a request for comment from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
Russia's Deputy Prime Minister Alexander Novak confirmed to Vesti news Oct. 30 that attacks on Russian refineries had been renewed, with three refineries targeted overnight.
"There was a pause for a while and today attacks on the refineries were renewed," he said in comments broadcast by Vesti.
There was limited damage at one of the refineries, Novak said, without specifying which one, adding that overall Russia's defense system had worked.
There were no confirmed reports of Ukraine attacking Russian refineries from Oct. 16 to Oct. 29, marking the longest strike-free period for Russia's refining system in more than seven months.
The pause in strikes on refineries followed major pressure from the US administration on Ukraine's President Volodymyr Zelenskyy. US President Donald Trump has repeatedly blamed Ukraine for keeping diesel prices high.
US military officials threatened to block intelligence sharing with Ukraine if the attacks on refineries did not stop, according to Zelenskyy. There were several instances of suspended intelligence sharing reported by Ukraine since then.
Russia partially lifted its diesel export ban Oct. 10 after the US

Treasury Department's Office of Foreign Assets Control granted a sanctions waiver to Russia Oct. 9, allowing the sale, delivery, and import of Russian-origin diesel fuel, including imports into the US.
-- Alexandra Vladimirova, Elza Turner

JAPAN DATA: Oil product exports fall first time in 3 months in September
Kerosene exports surge 319% on year Oil product imports drop for 16th straight month Naphtha shipments decline 40.3% in September

Japan's oil product exports fell 3.2% year over year in September to 486,919 barrels/day, marking the first decline in three months, according to preliminary data from the Ministry of Economy, Trade and Industry released Oct. 30.
Gasoline, naphtha, jet fuel and B/C-fuel oil exports fell 14.2%, 35.9%, 18.1% and 2.7% year over year to 48,116 b/d, 1,056 b/d, 133,580 b/d and 140,551 b/d, respectively, while kerosene, gasoil and A-fuel oil rose 319%, 7.5% and 63.3% to 20,640 b/d, 142,617 b/d and 358 b/d, respectively, in September, according to METI.
Meanwhile, Japan's oil product imports declined 34.8% year over year to 380,804 b/d in September, marking the 16th consecutive monthly decline, METI data showed.
Gasoline, naphtha and gasoil imports were down 22.2%, 40.3% and 73.1% to 80,642 b/d, 269,243 b/d and 6,745 b/d, respectively, while kerosene imports were up 325.7% to 10,580 b/d, according to METI.
METI publishes the data in kiloliters, which Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, converts to barrels at a conversion factor of 6.2898. Platts also takes into account METI's revisions to data published in previous months.

Sinopec expects high crude, shipping costs to pressure Q4 refining: Executives
Crude premiums, freight and insurance costs may exceed $20/b in Dec
Jan-Sept crude throughput down 10% YOY
Oil product export margin above $150/mt
Sinopec expects international crude prices and freight rates to remain elevated in the fourth quarter, putting significant pressure on its refining business, company executives said during Sinopec's Q3 briefing conference call on Oct. 30.
"We believe uncertainty remains over navigation through the Strait of Hormuz, while there are also no signs of easing in some other geopolitical conflicts," CFO Shou Donghua said. "We expect the international crude and shipping markets in Q4 to reflect a combination of supply-demand dynamics and geopolitical premiums, keeping prices at elevated levels."
Sinopec's preliminary forecast showed that crude procurement premiums, freight and insurance costs would remain at $15/b or above in the fourth quarter and could exceed $20/b in December, Shou said.
By comparison, crude procurement premiums, freight and insurance costs fell to about $4.29/b in Q3 from a peak of $14.74/b in Q2 when the Middle East conflict broke out, Shou said, adding that the decline boosted EBIT by 20.7 billion yuan.
The freight for a 270,000 mt VLCC carrying crude from the Arab Gulf to China averaged $245.27/mt as of Oct. 29 in Q4, according to data from Platts, part of S&P Global Energy.
Under China's current oil product pricing mechanism, introduced in 2013, the government adjusts retail ceiling prices for gasoline and gasoil every 10 working days based on a basket of benchmark crude prices, reportedly including ICE Brent, NYMEX WTI and DME Oman, with the components weighted according to China's crude import profile.
Market sources said freight and insurance were set at a combined $5/b in the formula, Platts reported.
Li Li, head of the Operation Management Department, said the company would continue to deepen cooperation with shipping companies, including actively pursuing equity cooperation with relevant shipping companies to strengthen its control over transportation.
On the procurement side, Sinopec would more flexibly adjust its mix of term and spot crude supplies while maintaining diversification in crude import sources, trading methods and, in particular, transportation methods, allowing the company to better time its crude purchases, Li said.
Refined product exports Li said Sinopec was still working out the detailed
arrangements for refined product exports in Q4. "Based on its current planning, the company expected its
available quotas to be sufficient to ensure stable domestic

refined product supplies while meeting its export needs," she said.
In terms of the pace of quota utilization, Sinopec has increased jet fuel and gasoil exports in response to movements in international oil prices, particularly changes in crack spreads, Li said.
Shou said Sinopec exported nearly 12.69 million mt of refined products, broadly stable year over year.
Sinopec has been awarded 17.6 million mt of clean oil product export quotas for 2026, up 4.2% year over year, Platts reported.
"Refined product export prices are generally aligned with the market, so our export margin should be above 1,000 yuan ($150.22)/mt," Shou said. "This has made a significant contribution to refining earnings."
Sinopec processed 170 million mt of crude in the first nine months, down 10.7% year over year, while refined product output fell 7.3% to 100 million mt.

Domestic ethylene-equivalent consumption fell 9.4% year over year in the first nine months, according to company data cited by Shou.
Shou said Sinopec had increased the share of higher-valueadded products, expanded chemical product exports, brought forward maintenance at units with low margins and strengthened overall cost controls.
The company also increased its use of lighter feedstocks such as ethane. Sinopec processed about 740,000 mt of ethane in January-September, compared with almost none in the same period last year, Shou said.
As a result, the chemicals segment recorded an EBIT loss of 1.7 billion yuan, narrowing its loss by 6.5 billion yuan year over year.
"We believe the decline in operating rates was due to raw material and supply shortages, which kept processing rates low," Shou said, adding that the overall decline in operating rates had an impact of nearly 3.6 billion yuan.

Refinery updates
REFINERY NEWS: Chevron boosts Q3 diesel output as Pascagoula hits record production
Refinery: Five US refineries; three joint-venture Asian refineries
Owner: Chevron
Overall capacity: 1 million b/d; 1.25 million b/d
Units affected: N/A
Duration: Q3 2026
Chevron ran its refineries at record levels in the third quarter, increasing diesel production amid a tight global market disrupted by conflict while managing rising freight costs, the company said Oct. 30.
Chevron reported record third-quarter crude throughput across its refining system as it looked to replace some of the 10% of global refining capacity offline.
Third-quarter crude throughputs at Chevron's US refining system averaged 1.093 million b/d, and international crude runs at 665,000 b/d, with the focus on distillate production.
"For our large refineries, our third quarter distillate yield in the United States was 46% and across our global portfolio was even higher than that," said Mark Nelson, Chevron's vice chairman in charge of Oil, Products, and Gas.
"In fact, Pascagoula hit a diesel production record at least over the last 3 or 4 years this quarter as well," he said.
Tweaking refinery assets Chevron, like many of its peers, is responding by extracting
more output from existing assets rather than relying solely on major expansions, to capitalize on margin strength.
"On the refining side, I would expect margins to stay elevated in the short term," Nelson said, without providing a timeline as to when that could be.
Margins should eventually moderate as refinery runs recover and export flows increase, but that recovery will take time, he added.
The company accelerated its debottlenecking project at its Pasadena, Texas, plant, increasing refinery output by about 15%. This project allows the plant to run increased volumes of Chevron's Permian production, which has remained above 1 million boe/d in Q3 2026 for the sixth consecutive quarter.
At Chevron's Pascagoula, Mississippi, plant -- its largest US Gulf Coast refinery -- the company expects a 7% capacity increase by 2030 through work spanning the next two turnaround cycles.

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

42

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 30, 2026

During Q3, the refinery recorded the highest diesel production "in at least three or four years," said Nelson.
Nelson said this was due to proprietary catalyst deployed at its largest refineries, combined with adjustments to distillation cut points, increasing distillate production by 10%, leveraging its substantial hydrocracking capacity.
Managing rising shipping costs Navigating logistical challenges for refineries now include
rising freight rates, particularly crude and refined product tankers, as changes in transportation are pushing up costs significantly, and been a "big mover here this last quarter," said Chevron CEO Mike Wirth on the call, adding that insurance is more expensive and takes longer.
"All that works its way from the refinery right back into the netback for your production," he added.
However, Wirth noted the advantage of having a controlled fleet, saying that a "significant portion" of its fleet is either ships owned by Chevron or ships under long-term time charter.
"These are contracts we entered into before the conflict began; they reflect charter rates before the conflict, which are significantly different than they are today. And so, we capture that transportation margin in our economics," he said.
Gross output from Chevron's three Venezuelan joint ventures is expected to reach about 300,000 b/d by year-end, Wirth said, up 20% over the last twelve months with plans to double output by 2031. Chevron refining system -- most notably Pascagoula and its El Segundo, California, refinery -- benefits from the availability of heavy crude.
Venezuelan crude discounts are surpassing those of its competitor, Western Canada Select, making it more attractive to USGC refiners.
According to data from Platts, Merey averaged a $25.24/b discount to WTI in the third quarter, versus $19.61/b for WCS exNederland--a $5.63/b wider discount for Merey. Fourth-quarterto-date, Merey is averaging a $28.22/b discount to WTI as of Oct. 30 versus $21.09/b for WCS ex-Nederland, making Merey's discount $7.13/b wider.
-- Janet Mcgurty

REFINERY NEWS: Valero to buy Lyondell's idled Houston plant; partially restart facility
Refinery: Houston, Texas
Owner: LyondellBasell
Overall capacity: 263,000 b/d
Units affected: N/A
Duration: End 2027
Valero will buy the former LyondellBasell Houston refinery, located adjacent to its own Houston plant, and partially restart the plant, the company said in a statement Oct. 30.
"This acquisition is a unique opportunity to strategically expand Valero's US Gulf Coast footprint at a fraction of replacement value and enhance the long-term strength of our refining system," Valero CEO Lane Riggs said in the statement.
"It is expected to provide a compelling financial return, consistent with Valero's disciplined capital allocation framework, while generating attractive commercial optimization opportunities and supporting future investment in high-complexity, heavy sour crude oil processing," he added.
The acquisition comes at a time of very tight global refining capacity and the restart of the light, sweet crude processing part of the plant will increase refined products output while being able to access Permian light sweet crude barrels.
Valero will pay $320.5 million for the refinery, which was shuttered in 2023. The transaction is expected to close by year-end 2026, subject to regulatory and other closing conditions.
Valero's initial plan is to bring back 165,000 b/d of light-sweet crude processing capacity by the end of 2027, with the potential to make a second-stage adjustment to run heavier crude through investment.
-- Janet Mcgurty

REFINERY NEWS: India's IOC Q2 throughput up 2.8%
Refinery: Nine fully owned
Owner: Indian Oil Corporation
Overall capacity: 70.3 million mt/year (1.4 million b/d)
India's No. 1 state-run refiner, Indian Oil Corp., saw refinery throughput rise 2.8% year over year to 18.11 million metric tons (1.44 million b/d) in the July-September period or Q2, the company said in a regulatory filing Oct. 30, reflecting uninterrupted processing in Asia's third-largest economy despite the Middle East conflict
In Q2, IOC's domestic sales rose 4.9% year over year to 23.98 million mt, it said.
Its exports fell 11.3% year over year to 1.25 million mt in Q2, it said.
IOC's fiscal year runs from April to March, according to the company.
IOC operates nine fully owned refineries with a combined capacity of 1.4 million b/d, the company said on its website.
IOC's Q2 profitability was largely driven by a temporary decline in crude oil prices from mid-June to mid-August and robust margins in petrochemicals and gas, the company said in a statement.
Profitability in upcoming quarters may be adversely impacted by inventory losses due to a potential fall in crude oil prices as the situation in the Middle East normalizes, IOC said.
Half-yearly results In the half-year period (April-September) or H1, IOC's refined
throughput rose 2.7% year year over year to a record 37.27 million mt, the company said.
In H1, IOC recorded the lowest-ever half-yearly Fuel and Loss of 8.28% post-Euro-VI scenario, it said.
In H1, IOC's half-yearly sales rose 2.9% to a new record 49.23 million mt, it said.
IOC sold 8.95 million mt of gasoline in H1, up 8.7% year over year, it said
It sold 19.86 mln mt of gas oil, up 7.9% year over year, it said. Exports fell 20.2% year over year to 2.21 million mt in H1, IOC said.
-- Ratnajyoti Dutta

REFINERY NEWS: Russia's Novokuybishev attacked overnight: reports
Refinery: Novokuybishev, Samara, Russia
Owner: Rosneft
Overall capacity: 164,000 b/d
Russia's Novokuybishev refinery, part of the Samara refinery hub, has been a target of Ukrainian drone strike overnight on Oct. 30, according to local media reports.
Russia's Deputy Prime Minister Alexander Novak said Oct. 30 that three refineries have been targeted overnight and that one sustained small damages.
Novak also said that the attacks on refineries have been renewed after a pause.
Novokuybishev has been the only refinery in the Samara hub operating normally, while the other two -- Syzran and Kuybishev -- remain offline after previous attacks, according to Russia-based market sources.
-- Elza Turner
REFINERY NEWS: Spain's Moeve to start Huelva biofuel unit H1 2027
Refinery: San Roque, Algeciras, Spain
Owner: Moeve
Overall capacity: 12.2 million mt/year, or 245,000 b/d
Refinery: La Rabida, Huelva, Spain
Owner: Moeve
Overall capacity: 11.2 million mt/year, or 225,000 b/d
Spain-based Moeve SA sees a new 500,000 metric tons/year biofuel unit at the Huelva refinery starting in the first half of 2027, CFO Carmen de Pablo said Oct. 30 during a conference call with analysts.
Moeve is owned by Mubadala Investment Company PJSC , with a 61% stake and The Carlyle Group Inc., with a 38% stake.
The company has, in recent months, signed a number of downstream supply deals to "create long-term demand" and a market for its product, de Pablo said. These include deals in the aviation, mining and steel sectors.
Current biofuel output is 1.3 million mt/y, according to company data.
The company also confirmed it has started construction of the first 300 MW phase of the Andalusian Green Hydrogen Valley at its Huelva refinery, the Onuba project, which it said is on track to meet its planned 2029 start up.
Moeve will lead and operate the project with a 51% stake. The project has secured up to 304 million of the Spanish government funding and has been recognized as a project of common interest.
Moeve said Oct. 8 that it had agreed a supply deal for renewable energy from two solar PV plants in the region with
