The focus on the two large-scale projects means Moeve dedicated 69% of its capital expenditure to low-carbon transformation during the first nine months, compared to 52% in the year-ago period, it said.
Capital expenditure totaled a record 885m in the first nine months, up 17% year over year.
Looking ahead, Moeve expects its refining margin to be supported in the upcoming months by a tight middle distillate market and low inventories, but overall soften due to a "higher crude premium market" and increasing freight costs, De Pablo said, without providing an estimate.
The company reported a refining margin of $26.80/b in the third quarter, and its two refineries recorded 100% utilization, de Pablo said. The margin benefited from tight product market and rising fuel demand in Spain, while Moeve used its system flexibility to boost kerosene and diesel output, de Pablo said.
Combined refinery throughput in Q3 totaled 5.6 million mt, according to the preliminary figures, up 6% year over year and up 12% quarter over quarter.
The company did not report whether it has upcoming or ongoing maintenance.

ExxonMobil maximizes diesel output, sees prolonged product-market tightness
Refinery: Five US refineries
Owner: ExxonMobil
Overall capacity: 1.96 million b/d
Units affected: plant
Duration: Q3
ExxonMobil continues to maximize diesel production as it continues with upgrades to its US Gulf Coast refineries begun before the current tightness in the global diesel market, company executives said on Oct. 30.
With expectations of tight refined product markets well into at least 2027, ExxonMobil's signature countercyclical investment strategies have paid off, with projects like "Growing the Gulf" already underway.
"We've been really on this journey of transforming the competitiveness of our refining circuit by investing selectively and refineries, which have scale, which have an integrated portfolio of high-value products," said CEO Darren Woods on the third quarter results call.
"And the organization has now been very focused on how can we continue to high grade our products late and improve the yields across those refineries," he added.

ExxonMobil's diesel production so far this year averaged roughly 1.1 million b/d, with its three US Gulf Coast refineries operating at about 100% crude utilization rates in Q3 as the company looked to maximize output to meet shortages, helped by a 3.5% higher diesel yield in the quarter compared with 2019 levels.
The company expects Gulf Coast reconfiguration projects to increase distillate output by about 100,000 b/d.
A first phase is underway at Baytown, Texas, with further investments planned at Baton Rouge, Louisiana, and Beaumont, Texas. The projects, some of which were begun before the coronavirus, are intended to increase production of diesel, lubricants and chemical feedstocks, reflecting a broader effort to improve the product mix rather than simply expand crude throughput.
Rebuilding and restarting As to how long the current refined product tightness could
continue, Woods said he didn't have a line of sight as to how or when depleted global inventories of refined products would be replenished or when elevated refining cracks would play out, given the different variables at play.
"But I think the first thing that's ultimately going to have to happen is the world's capacity has to get back to where it was prior to these conflicts," he said,
Conflicts have damaged refineries and getting those repaired and product moving out of them consistently will "be a critical piece of the equation," he said, referring in part to damage inflicted on Russia's refineries in its war with Ukraine.
"I'd say one-third of the shortage out there roughly is just the inability to get clean products out of the Middle East and into the world markets," he added.
"Then the third area where there's an opportunity is China, which has got capacity they could bring online and run at a little higher rate to put more exports out there. I think that's the other piece of the equation that can help bring markets back into balance. So I think those are the three variables that I see difficult to predict any one of those, frankly," Woods said.
"My guess would be it will take certainly well through next year before we see things getting back closer to normal, but it could be longer than that, frankly," he added.
-- Janet Mcgurty

Refinery: Slavyansk (Slavyansky, Slavinsky), Krasnodar, Russia

Assessments continue at Russia's Slavyansk refinery, aimed to establish the necessary repairs of the damaged equipment, the refinery said Oct. 30 in a Telegram post.

Platts Brazil domestic refined products publishing schedule for Nov. 2
Please note that S&P Global Energy's São Paulo office will be closed on Nov. 2 for the All Souls' Day (Finados) holiday in Brazil.
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will not publish price assessments for Brazil domestic refined product markets, or Brazil diesel and gasoline market commentaries on this day.
Latin American Wire will be published without Brazil domestic prices on Nov. 2. The Platts Brazil Fuels Daily market report will publish on Nov. 2 with information from Oct. 30, and run again on Nov. 4 with information from Nov. 3.
Fixed pages 355, 1631 and 1639 of Platts Global Alert (PGA) and Platts Refined Products Alert (PRF) will not be published on Nov. 2.
Other Latin American assessments and Brazil DAP cargo assessments for diesel and gasoline will be published as normal.
Normal publishing for Brazil domestic refined assessments and commentaries will resume on Tuesday, Nov. 3.
For full details of the S&P Global Energy publishing schedule and services affected, refer to Holiday Publication Schedule.
For queries, please contact support@platts.com.
Platts to amend rollover date of Asian Dated Brent Strip at London close
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the rollover date of its Asian Dated Brent (ADB) Strip at London close assessment to the first publication day of each month, effective Dec. 1, 2026.
The change follows a review of historical data sets and will align the ADB Strip at London close assessment with the current rollover date of the ADB Strip at Asia close assessment.

Currently, this assessment, which reflects the forward assessment of Dated Brent two months ahead of the date of publication at the 4:30 pm London close, rolls over on the 9th of the month.
Platts proposed this change in a subscriber note published Sept. 21, 2026.

Platts to launch STS Lome Gasoline differentials to Eurobob FOB AR assessments
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch a new daily price assessment reflecting the differential between the Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment, effective Nov. 23, 2026.
This follows increased demand for transparency on West African gasoline differentials, as Nigeria's Dangote refinery continues to reshape flows and pricing dynamics in the Atlantic Basin.

The assessed differential is calculated as the difference between the physical Gasoline STS Lome assessment and the midpoint of the three-to-10-day forward strip, itself derived from the established Gasoline Eurobob FOB AR barge assessment derivative assessments.
The new assessments will provide a transparent indication of the value of STS Lome gasoline relative to Northwest European gasoline blendstock assessments, and will complement Platts existing outright STS Lome gasoline differentials against Platts Premium 10ppm FOB Mediterranean cargoes and Premium 10ppm FOB Rotterdam barges.

Platts to amend freight calculation for Oman, Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will amend the freight calculation used in its daily Oman and Murban CFR North Asia crude assessments, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
Under the change, Platts will use the daily VLCC Gulf of Omanto-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (DOOEW00), which was launched Aug. 3, in its calculation of the outright price of Oman and Murban delivered into North Asia at 4:30 pm Singapore time (0830 GMT).
The change will better reflect the shipping economics for the two grades, with Murban primarily loading from Fujairah port and Oman from Mina Al Fahal port outside the Strait of Hormuz.
Currently, the freight calculation is based on the daily VLCC Persian Gulf-to-Far East freight assessment in $/mt (TDAAB00).
Platts first proposed the change in a subscriber note published Sept. 28, 2026.
The freight calculation will continue to use the conversion factor for metric tons to barrels, as applied to the grades, in line with their prevailing qualities. The conversion factors are

Platts amends Abu Dhabi crude OSPs publication Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, has updated its publication
of Abu Dhabi crude official selling prices effective Oct. 8, 2026, in line with changes announced by Abu Dhabi National Oil Co. (ADNOC).
In July, ADNOC said it would adopt a prompt-month pricing methodology based on the Platts Dubai benchmark (PCAAT00) plus an ADNOC-announced differential for the OSPs of its Abu Dhabi onshore and offshore crude grades, with effect from November 2026 loadings. ADNOC added that it would announce the OSP differentials for Murban, Das, Umm Lulu and Upper Zakum in the month prior to the delivery month.
Accordingly, Platts will publish the monthly OSP differentials for the four grades, basis Platts Dubai, beginning with the November differentials issued by ADNOC on Oct. 8, 2026.
Platts first announced the change in a subscriber note published on Aug. 31, 2026.
The OSP differentials are published under the following reinstated codes:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

OSP Diff to Dubai AAIOS00 OSLUD00 AAXPL00 AAIOV00

Platts previously published the OSP differentials under these codes until May 2021, when they were then suspended due to a change in the ADNOC pricing methodology.
From November, the existing codes for the outright OSPs for Murban (AAIKZ00), Upper Zakum (AAILC00), Das Blend (AAXPH00) and Umm Lulu (OSLUF00) will remain unchanged. Platts will publish the outright OSPs of the four grades following the publication of the average of Platts Dubai crude assessments during the month of loading.
In view of the changes, Platts is no longer updating the codes reflecting Upper Zakum, Das Blend and Umm Lulu OSP differentials against Murban, as well as the codes for all four grades, including Murban, calculated against the monthly average of Platts cash Dubai 1st Line assessment in the month of trade.
The following codes will no longer be updated from October:

Grade Murban Umm Lulu Das Blend Upper Zakum

Diff to Dubai AOSPA00 AOSPB00 AOSPC00 AOSPD00

Diff to Murban OSP
AOSPE00 AOSPF00 AOSPG00

ADNOC's OSPs are published in the Platts price database and on Platts Global Alert page 1051.
Please send all feedback or questions to asia_crude@spglobal.com and pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if they are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make those not marked as confidential available upon request.

Platts to launch daily FOB Middle East bitumen assessments from Nov. 2
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, will launch daily VG30 and VG40 bitumen spot assessments for FOB Middle East, effective Nov. 2, 2026.
The new assessments will complement Platts' existing daily and weekly bitumen assessments, providing greater price transparency and information in the waterborne trade for the product in the Asia-Pacific and Middle East markets.
Platts will launch two new daily outright assessments and their respective monthly averages. These assessments will reflect bulk viscosity grade 30 and VG40 grade bitumen, as per IS 73:2013 specifications, loading 10-30 days forward from the date of publication in the Middle East.

Assessment FOB Middle East Bitumen VG30 FOB Middle East Bitumen VG40

Quantity

Laycan

2,500-10,000 mt, bulk 10-30 days forward

UoM $/mt

2,500-10,000 mt, bulk 10-30 days forward $/mt

Pricing information for material with other specifications, such as PEN 60/70, may be considered for the assessments, normalized to the proposed standard.
The assessments will reflect market value at the close of Asian trading at 4:30 pm Singapore time and follow the Singapore publication schedule. Bids, offers and trades reported throughout the day may be considered in the assessment process, with values normalized to the market close at 4:30 pm Singapore time.
This follows a proposal published Sept. 14 and available here. Please send any questions, comments or feedback to asia_products@spglobal.com, with a copy to pricegroup@spglobal.com. For written comments, please provide a clear indication if they are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make those not marked as confidential available upon request.

Platts revises measurement units, descriptions for FO Licht ethanol prices
The following Platts FOLicht ethanol prices have had their units of measurement and descriptions updated, as summarized below.
Cubic Meter Pure Alcohol (CPA) · Type: Volume Unit · Definition: A unit of volume that measures the volume of pure (100%) alcohol. One CBMPA equals 1 cubic meter (1,000 liters) of pure alcohol. Unlike a standard cubic meter (m³), which measures the total volume of any substance (liquid, gas, or solid), CBMPA refers specifically to the ethanol content, not the total liquid volume.

Platts proposes to launch weighted indexes for Chicago CBOB, ULSD
Platts, part of S&P Global Energy, is proposing to launch Chicago basket indexes for gasoline and diesel from March 2, incorporating the independent assessments for the major pipelines and Buckeye Complex and weighting them by trading volume, as well as adding a new, separate assessment for Chicago West Shore and Badger pipeline ULSD.
Feedback from market participants has indicated the need for Chicago-weighted basket indexes that reflect recent volumes traded on the Badger, West Shore, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex.
The proposed weightings would be calculated by tracking recent trades across the West Shore, Badger, and Wolverine pipelines, as well as the Buckeye Complex. Total reported trades would be compiled on a cyclical basis, assuming a minimum volume of 10,000 barrels where not specified. These trades will then be sorted by pipeline to determine the percentage contribution of each component.
The weighted index would be calculated from the average of the differentials to NYMEX futures for the West Shore/Badger

© 2026 by S&P Global Inc. All rights reserved.

©2026 S&P Global Inc. Any unauthorized use of this copyrighted material constitutes infringement of our intellectual property and will subject you to possible legal action.

Platts Oilgram Price Report

October 30, 2026

pipelines, Wolverine pipeline and BCX assessments, adjusted by their corresponding weightings.
For example, if Platts is assessing Cycle 2 for Chicago ULSD and traded volumes from the previous cycles came out to 20% West Shore and Badger, 30% Wolverine and 50% Buckeye Complex and the pipeline differentials were assessed at plus 2 cents/gal, plus 3 cents/gal and plus 3.50 cents/gal, respectively, the index basket differential would be plus 3.05 cents/gal.
Platts' current assessments for Chicago CBOB and ULSD are as follows:

Symbol AATHA00 AGCWC00 ACBAE00 AAREL00 ACBAA00 AGCWA00

Name ULSD Chicago pipeline ULSD Chicago Wolverine Pipeline ULSD Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline Gasoline CBOB Chicago BCX Gasoline CBOB Chicago Wolverine Pipeline

The proposed Chicago basket indexes would be as follows:
Name Gasoline CBOB Chicago Basket ULSD Chicago Basket
Platts is concurrently seeking feedback on how to incorporate West Shore and Badger into the weighted index when the 1 psi waiver is in place during the summer. More information on how Platts will adjust the West Shore and Badger assessments in

accordance with the 1 psi waiver can be found here. Additionally, Platts seeks feedback on the naming conventions
for the proposal. Platts proposes to amend the assessment name from Gasoline CBOB Chicago pipeline to Gasoline CBOB West Shore and Badger pipeline to better represent the products assessed. This change follows the Feb. 3, 2025, removal of the Wolverine Pipeline from the basket underpinning the generic Chicago assessment due to the anticipated removal of the 1 psi waiver in multiple Midwest states and a division in RVP.
Platts is also proposing to launch a separate West Shore/ Badger assessment for Chicago ULSD; this would not replace the existing generic assessment, which would remain unchanged. The name of the new assessment would be ULSD Chicago West Shore and Badger Pipeline.
The indexes would appear on PGA pages 350, 351, 154, 24, 482, 410 and 411, as well as in US Marketscan, Oilgram Price Report, and on Platts Connect.
Please send all feedback and comments by Jan. 23 to americas_products@spglobal.com and pricegroup@spglobal.com.
For written comments, please provide a clear indication if comments are not intended for publication by Platts for public viewing. Platts will consider all comments received and will make comments not marked as confidential available upon request.

3-DAY TRAINING

Oil Markets Fundamentals & Commercial Strategies

Nov 3-5, 2026 | Singapore

Dec 8-10, 2026 | Dubai

Mar 23-25, 2027 | Singapore Apr 6-8, 2027 | Houston

Apr 27-29, 2026 | London

Oil markets are increasingly complex, shaped by shifting supply and demand, geopolitical developments, changing trade flows and price volatility. This course provides a practical understanding of the forces shaping global crude oil and refined product markets.
Explore market fundamentals, price formation and forecasting, trading, and risk management, and learn how to translate market developments into better commercial decisions.
Build the market knowledge and commercial insight to make more confident decisions in a rapidly evolving oil market.
WHO SHOULD ATTEND The course would be suitable for:
· Technical and non-technical professionals working across crude oil and refined products
· Traders, brokers and market analysts · Professionals in commercial, planning, business development,
supply and distribution roles · Shipping and logistics professionals · New industry entrants seeking a practical understanding of oil
markets

Upstream Fundamentals and Economics

Fossil fuels dominate the energy mix representing over 80% of global energy demand.
